# Copy corrections

Every student-facing wording fix, with the reason it was wrong. Read this before
writing or editing copy for LSAT U so the same mistake is not made twice.

Companion to [[teaching-voice]], which says how the writing should sound. This
says what has actually gone wrong and why.

Started 2026-09-07 from a Read, Recall, Colour pass. Add to it every time a
correction is made.

## The rules these corrections came from

Three rules already existed and all three leaked anyway, because each was
enforced on the components that happened to exist when it was written. A surface
added later starts uncovered.

- **No em dashes, ever.** [[teaching-voice]], Core voice.
- **No internal vocabulary in student copy.** `projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`
  carries the glossary and marks what to avoid.
- **Every CSS token a stylesheet uses must be defined in it.** `projects/lsatu/CLAUDE.md`.

Guards now live with the content rather than with each component:
`content/instructions-voice.test.ts` checks the authored instructions, so every
surface that renders them is covered, including surfaces not written yet.

## Corrections

### Contrapositive and arrow lessons: cut the sophistry (2026-10-11)

Joshua rejected the first contrapositive lesson as 'terribly done' and then rejected the rewrite for 'a lot of sophistry'. Rules from the two rounds:

- **Say what it is first, in plain words, before any example.** Name the idea, say what it states, then show it. The contrapositive is the **same rule said a different way**. 29	### Contrapositive and arrow lessons: cut the sophistry (2026-10-11)
30	
31	Joshua rejected the first contrapositive lesson as 'terribly done' and then rejected the rewrite for 'a lot of sophistry'. Rules from the two rounds:
32	
33	- **Say what it is first, in plain words, before any example.** Name the idea, say what it states, then show it. The contrapositive is the **same rule said a different way**. The two are **logically equivalent**: if one is true, the other is true, and you can use either. Lead with that. Source: his own teaching, [[contrapositive-teaching]].
34	- **No metaphor-of-obligation language.** Never write that a rule 'makes a promise', that the left side 'depends on' the right, or that it has 'nothing to stand on'. Say what is true and why, in the plainest reasoning: if the left side had happened, the right side would have come with it.
35	- **Keep it natural, intuitive and personable.** Contractions, short sentences, a teacher thinking next to the student. No lecture-hall phrasing. If a sentence sounds like it is trying to sound wise, cut it.
36	- **Name the lesson by its topic.** 'Contrapositive', not 'Write and read the contrapositive'.
37	- **A lesson explains before it drills.** Order for a concept lesson: what it is, see it work, how to do it, what it is not, the steps in short, why it matters. Do not open with an example or a question.
38	- **Must arrow note must say:** what the arrow says, where to start reading (the left), and what the arrow is for. Not just which side is sufficient.
39	- **A card front asks the question, the back gives the answer first.** The answer is the large line. Be specific: 'Can you read a rule from right to left?', not 'Can you reverse the arrow?'. Never 'the other one'.
40	- **Fix the vague word, not the sentence.** 'Reverse' and 'the other one' were both rejected as not specific. Say what the student actually does or what the condition actually is.
41	- **Never call the contrapositive 'the one other thing' a rule tells you.** Joshua (2026-10-11): it is not the only other thing you know. Say the two are equivalent and interchangeable: know one, you know the other. Do not claim a closed list of what else is known.
42	- **Do not use the raffle (win the raffle, get a bicycle) as the example in new contrapositive or arrow material.** Pick a fresh everyday example for each lesson. 67	### Conditional opening: define the words before using them (2026-10-03)
68	
69	Joshua's marked browser paragraphs used “direction” and “guarantee” before making their meaning clear. The purpose paragraph also described an argument accepting more than its information supports without showing what that meant. Teach what a rule lets you conclude in plain language, then attach the vocabulary to a concrete relationship. Explain sufficient as enough and necessary as required before relying on those roles. Show what the must-arrow records and why English clause order does not determine the two sides.
70	
71	2026-10-03 clarification: explicitly distinguish the indicator from the complete condition it refers to. Sufficient goes left; necessary goes right. The indicator itself is not a condition in the diagram. Show the same relationship with if at the beginning and in the middle, and show necessary targets both before and after requirement language. Neither sentence position nor the words immediately following the indicator determine placement. Preserve if versus only if as different complete expressions.
72	
73	A rule describes what would follow **if** a condition were true; it does not say that condition happened. Bold that “if” and use meaningful in-view motion to expose the connection, with a static reduced-motion equivalent. Replace compressed ready-order wording with a natural full sentence. Give each paragraph and interaction a specific teaching job. These corrections supplement the canonical voice standard rather than adding a new writing register.
74	
75	### Generic reasoning prompts → concrete numbered demonstrations (2026-09-30)
76	
77	Joshua marked four parts of the V6 opening: "Read each whole idea," "Find what the statements guarantee," the abstract first sentence about information, and the paragraph combining application and linking. They named goals without showing a useful action or explanation. Replace them with numbered, separate scenarios. Show the fact and rule that prove a conclusion about one case; separately show two matching rules and their new conditional conclusion. Labels should identify actual inputs or outputs. Review the whole note for repeated umbrella instructions and make task instructions executable. This follows the teaching voice: specific, concrete, short sentences, with air between distinct ideas.
78	
79	### Fact and rule definitions must explain the inference (2026-09-30)
80	
81	Joshua rejected definitions that remained abstract and an explanation that did not clearly distinguish applying a rule from linking rules. Two connecting rules produce another conditional rule without establishing a case exists. Adding a fact to the chain is a separate step. Explain all four fact situations and a hidden chain revealed by contraposition rather than leaving them implicit in an interactive model. Rules include general logical relationships, not only policies or obligations.

174	| "Read one passage instead" | "Or read one passage now" |
175	| "Open analytics" | "See where you lose points" |
176	| "Open journal" | "Review your missed questions" |
177	
178	"Smart Drill" is the product's internal name for the adaptive set. On a button it
179	asks the student to guess what she is about to get. The sentence above the button
180	still names it, which is where a product name belongs: after the thing has been
181	explained, not as the explanation.
182	
183	"Instead" was the worse one. It tells the student she has just declined the
184	assigned work by reading past it. She has not. "Or" offers a second path without
185	narrating a decision she never made.
186	
187	"Open analytics" and "Open journal" name a screen. A student does not want
188	analytics, she wants to know where the points are going.
189	
190	The fallback paragraph lost "an adaptive set that follows your weakest question
191	types" for "the question types you miss most": the same fact, without the product
192	vocabulary.
193	
194	**Lesson:** a button says what happens next, in the student's words. Feature names
195	belong in the sentence that explains them, never on the control.
196	
197	## Words never to use in student copy
198	
199	From the glossary. Internal names may stay in code, never on the page.
200	
201	| Never | Use instead |
202	|---|---|
203	| beat, beats | step |
204	| chunk | piece |
205	| intake | plain instruction |
206	| briefing, phase, tab, card | the thing's own name |
207	| say it back | tell it back |
208	| The Read | The Recall |
209	| Recall Rephrase Colour | Read, Recall, Colour |
210	| em dash, en dash | a full stop, or a colon before a list |
211	| expression ("Which expression identifies...") | "indicators" for the taught words, "phrase" for a multi-word indicator read as one unit ("read only if as one phrase"), "idea" or "condition" for what it points to. Added 2026-10-11: "expression" is vague and stiff; the student is looking for an indicator, so name it and reinforce the term being taught |
212	| statement ("Statement 1 of 7") | sentence. Added 2026-10-11: students read sentences; "statement" sounds like a logic textbook |
213	| Practise / "N statements" on drill cards | say what the drill has the student do. Added 2026-10-11 |
214	| establish ("enough to establish") | prove, or tells you. Added 2026-10-11: plainer; keep "guarantee", it is plain and sticks |
215	| preserve ("preserves the rule") | keeps. Added 2026-10-11 |
216	| relationship (when a rule is meant) | rule. Added 2026-10-11 |
217	| stipulated | this rule. Added 2026-10-11 |
218	| classify | sort. Added 2026-10-11 |
219	| "That keeps the meaning." on every correct step | name what was right: "Right indicator.", "Right direction."; wrong: "Not quite. Try again." Added 2026-10-11 |
220	| Personalized If/Then | Put yourself in it. Added 2026-10-11: names what the student does, not a feature |
221	| target ("the sufficient target") | what it points to, or the group. Added 2026-10-11; "target" stays in code |
222	| the model ("compare with the model") | our version. Added 2026-10-11 |
223	| "has to be true for it" (legend) | "required for the result". Added 2026-10-11: "it" had nothing to refer to; every label must make sense on its own |
224	| wrong-answer feedback that explains the right answer | a hint about why the chosen answer fails ("“only if” introduces what is required, not what is enough"). Added 2026-10-11: the explanation gave the answer away |
225	| "Build the arrow" | "Build the diagram". Added 2026-10-11: the student builds a diagram; the arrow is one symbol in it |
226	| "Select the words that signal the rule" | "Select the conditional indicator." Added 2026-10-11: name the thing the student is looking for |
227	| "28 sentences", "Sentence 1 of 28", "Next sentence" | questions: "28 questions", "Question 1 of 28", "Next question". Added 2026-10-11: a sentence is not the unit of a drill and reads unprofessional |
228	| "Drill 1" as a title | the task itself, e.g. "Spot the condition". Added 2026-10-11: a number says nothing about the reasoning |
229	| "a rule makes a promise", "depends on", "nothing to stand on", "the other one" | say what is true and why, in plain words. Added 2026-10-11: reads as sophistry; vague words like "the other one" point at nothing |
230	| "Right." on a correct answer | "Correct." Added 2026-10-11 |
231	
232	**Before designing any new homework, bootcamp, drill or note, read this table.** When a
233	word is rejected in review, add it here first, with the reason, then fix the copy.
234	
235	## Conditional Rephrase: name the student action, not the internal model (2026-09-15)
236	
237	**Cut:** “record the route,” “choose the route,” and a map presented as five rules.
238	
239	**Keep:** diagram the statement on paper, choose the matching diagram, and organize
240	the map into four groups. “Required means right” is a separate takeaway because it
241	explains placement; it is not a fifth group.
242	
243	“Route” described the system’s representation, not the student’s work. Calling five
244	unlike ideas rules also flattened the teaching structure and made the layout harder to
245	read.
246	
247	**Lesson:** name the action the student can see herself doing. Do not force a teaching
248	reminder into the same hierarchy as the categories it explains.
249	
250	Related: [[teaching-voice]] · [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]] · [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]]
251	
252	## Session recaps: write a reusable reference, not a play-by-play (2026-09-12)
253	
254	**Cut:** an opener saying the student got two questions right, followed by a summary of
255	which answer they chose and how they explained it.
256	
257	**Keep:** the concept the student needs tomorrow, a visual model of the reasoning, a
258	short diagnostic check, and the exact practice they need to complete.
259	
260	A student will not remember an isolated answer-choice exchange a day later, and the
261	exchange is not useful unless it demonstrates a reusable rule. A recap should read like
262	a compact study note, not a record of the conversation. Name a question only when the
263	details make the concept easier to apply. Praise belongs only when it identifies a
264	durable skill or meaningful change, not merely that an answer was correct.
265	
266	**Lesson:** organize the recap around what the student can use next. Lead with the
267	concept, show the logic clearly, and finish with tangible practice.
268	
269	### A rule is not a fact (2026-09-12)
270	
271	**Cut:** describing a conditional Must Be True lesson as “applying a rule to a fact”
272	without checking whether the stimulus supplied a fact at all.
273	
274	**Keep:** a fact is an asserted true case or condition. A rule is a general conditional
275	relationship. It tells you what follows if its trigger is present, but it does not say
276	that the trigger occurred. Some stimuli contain rules only, so the work is to chain
277	them. When a fact is present, apply a matching rule or a valid contrapositive.
278	
279	**Lesson:** label the role of each sentence before explaining the inference. Never
280	invent a fact to make a rule fire.
281	
282	### A post-session note is lecture material, not an email (2026-09-12)
283	
284	**Cut:** a greeting, a dated recap heading, references to a question the student is
285	expected to remember, and a sign-off.
286	
287	**Keep:** a concept title, a clear opening distinction, numbered procedures, spaced
288	examples, and practice that uses the lesson.
289	
290	**Lesson:** the note should remain useful when the session itself is no longer fresh.
291	It must teach from the page instead of relying on remembered context.
292	
293	The concept should not arrive as an abrupt list. Begin with a short explanation of
294	why the distinction matters, show the full method at a glance, and then teach each
295	path in the same order. Use tables for comparisons and numbered headings for a
296	procedure so the student can see the structure before reading the detail.
297	
298	### Define the concept in Joshua's language (2026-09-28)
299	
300	**Cut:** “Some, most, and all tell you how much of a group fits a description.”
301	
302	**Keep:** “Some means at least one. Most means more than half. All means every one,”
303	grounded in a concrete set of ten cookies before drawing conclusions from the words.
304	
305	The cut line is vague and does not sound like Joshua teaching. It makes the reader
306	decode “fits a description” before learning any of the three words. It also risks
307	blurring the difference between a count that is possible and one that is guaranteed.
308	
309	**Lesson:** open with the actual distinction or definition Joshua taught. Use a
310	concrete example to show what each statement proves, then introduce notation and
311	the inferences. Do not write an abstract umbrella sentence merely to announce the
312	topic.
313	
314	
315	### Reference quality does not authorize reference topics (2026-09-30)
316	
317	Joshua requested the Some, Most, and All note as a teaching-flow and visual-quality reference. Quantity examples and exercises do not belong in the separate facts/rules note. Remove that off-topic material; retain the distinction between what a rule says and what a fact establishes. Before future recaps, read the accumulated corrections in the Post Session Recap skill's `references/recap-calibration.md`. Preserve source-driven scope while matching the reference's teaching quality.
318	
319	### Conditional practice: explain the decision instead of naming the operation (2026-10-07)
320	
321	Joshua rejected “same rule, different words,” “equivalent-rule comparison,” “find the requirement, then place it,” “work through the expressions below,” “first choose the complete target,” “read the whole idea,” and “sentence X of Y” when these stood in for a useful explanation or made the drill feel like a collection of labeled sections. “Complete target” and similar terminology should not carry a beginner's instructions without a concrete meaning attached.
322	
323	The revised local copy asks what the learner needs to decide: “Who does the rule say will receive a wristband?”, “What kind of key is needed to open the cabinet?”, and “What kind of pass does entry require?” The lesson demonstrates why before noon, current, registered and with yellow tags matter. Dropping one of those words changes which cases the rule covers. An arrow is checked by imagining its left condition and asking what the sentence then establishes, rather than treating the first English clause as the left side.
324	
325	Joshua also asked for the mixed drill to train a skill and build a habit, with one question visible and a progress bar. The current local response uses five stages with a concrete purpose for each: retain the full conditions, build a diagram, attempt a translation before supplied labels, compare meanings, then transfer to unfamiliar cases. Error feedback distinguishes dropped qualifications, reversed roles, required versus enough and changed meaning. A fresh question checks the repaired distinction; correcting the exposed answer alone does not demonstrate transfer. Written translations are compared by the learner and are not represented as automatically graded.
326	
327	Keep modality in the examples. An electric vehicle that is denied permission disproves the claim that being electric guarantees permission; a vehicle merely staying outside does not. A required action is an obligation, not a fact proving compliance. Confirmation practice for that mistake must test another obligation rather than an unrelated wording comparison.
328	
329	These are demonstrated corrections and the current implementation response, not approval of every exact replacement sentence or proof that the voice matches Joshua identically. The [selected voice standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md) still governs wording, cadence, concrete reasoning and scope. [Current session record](log.md) distinguishes local work from publication.
330	
331	Further steering in the same session: “Keep the complete condition” was itself awkward as a visible heading. Ask who or what the particular rule covers instead. Joshua also rejected the landing page's total question/stage counts, per-stage counts, long stage list and padded explanation of repairs. A shorter launch should explain the immediate task; each stage should introduce its purpose and instructions when the learner reaches it. That latest flow revision was requested while final verification was still pending, so this record does not certify its implementation.
332	
333	
334	### RC actions should describe what the student gets (2026-10-07)
335	
336	Joshua rejected Inspect set as unclear and awkward. Use Preview drill for viewing the exact selected passages and questions before starting. Keep Start this drill distinct and explain whether answers are hidden and whether work is saved. Generic Menu is also insufficient where the actual action is Question details. This correction does not justify adding more launch instructions, repeated summaries, or unrelated assigned work inside RC setup.
337	
338	
339	
340	### Unless: choose either idea (2026-10-12)
341	
342	Joshua rejected "Find the idea that is not attached to unless." Teach the Group 3 move as: choose either idea, negate it, put it on the left; take the other idea and put it on the right exactly as written. Do not single out which idea to pick. His answer key happens to negate the non-unless idea; choosing the other gives the contrapositive, which is also correct. Applied in the LSAT U Unless drill (PR #205).
343	
344	## 2026-10-13: Necessary condition, his definition and the door
345	
346	- **Approved wording (Joshua, 2026-10-13):** "The necessary condition is what you can guarantee will occur if the sufficient condition happens." Keep verbatim on the Necessary page.
347	- **Door is allowed as a careful metaphor** (sufficient condition opens the door, necessary condition is guaranteed on the other side). Still never "door" as a unit of content. Keep it accurate: the door image must not imply the necessary condition only exists behind it, or that it causes or follows in time.
348	- **Avoid anything that sounds AI-written.** Plain sentences, no flourishes.
349	
350	## 2026-10-13: Spot the Relationship comparisons and explanations
351	
352	The activity rejected implied comparisons in questions 22 and 28 while accepting them in 31, 36 and 47. A comparison can use another object, a numeric benchmark or an earlier state clear from context. Do not teach that it always needs two named objects or the word *than*. The repaired examples remove ambiguous comparative wording from the early single-type questions rather than asking students to ignore it. Question 47 compares pressure with a pressure threshold, not flux.
353	
354	Questions 33–48 now explain what each indicator connects, the complete requirement or comparison, and the causal reason. A relationship count alone is not an explanation. Several comparisons can share one relationship type; distinguish the number of types from the number of claims. These are implementation corrections under Joshua's fix request, not approval of every exact replacement sentence.
355	======
356	# Bootcamp teaching preferences
357	
358	Captured 2026-10-11 from Joshua's conditional-bootcamp conversation. These are requirements for future builds where applicable, not proof that any implementation has passed acceptance.
359	
360	## General standards confirmed by repeated corrections
361	
362	- Compare the topic against internal corpus sources before drafting. Preserve source provenance and distinguish a source claim from Joshua's decision. Ingest new clips through the ingest skill before relying on them. Never infer a video transcript from its title.
363	- Explain the concept in plain language, then develop it through a concrete familiar example. Introduce notation after meaning. A short definition alone is not a lesson. Avoid recursive definitions and tongue twisters.
364	- Break explanations into readable sections with clear visual hierarchy, larger text, generous spacing, and short paragraphs. Inspect the actual rendered page, including the docked browser width and mobile, rather than treating JSX inspection as visual approval.
365	- Bring meaningful interaction early, after enough teaching to attempt it. Feedback explains the reasoning and distinguishes given facts from conclusions of a rule. Do not merely cycle reveal buttons and call that practice.
366	- Use color, emphasis, and motion to direct attention to the entire relevant idea. Preserve complete conditions, restrictions, timing, modality, and the difference between a rule and a fact.
367	- Preserve existing teaching media while revising. Check that videos still exist and play in their intended lesson. New media must earn its place in the teaching flow.
368	- Review content against the corpus, review the rendered UI, and use independent Claude Code review when requested. Record the actual model from the run, review artifact, findings, disposition, and limitations. Code/screenshot review is not live browser testing. Tests, local build, deployment, and signed-in acceptance are separate claims.
369	
370	## Necessary-condition lesson: explicitly approved wording
371	
372	Joshua supplied and approved this copy on 2026-10-11:
373	
374	> **A necessary condition is a requirement. It has to be met, but meeting it might not be enough.**
375	> For example, you have to be a student to be enrolled in law school. That makes being a student **necessary for law-school enrollment**.
376	> But being a student doesn’t mean you’re enrolled in law school. You could be studying history instead.
377	> **Being a student is required. It doesn’t establish enrollment on its own.**
378	
379	Keep “might not”: a condition may be both necessary and sufficient. Do not replace this with “something another condition can’t be true without.”
380	
381	Explicit page corrections:
382	- Emphasize the complete consequent, “then you are a student,” and identify “you are a student” as the condition. Do not teach isolated “then” as a standalone necessary-condition indicator.
383	- Law student / history student / not a student should be vertical case tabs. Keep keyboard navigation, visible selected state, and mobile stacking. This is a decision for this case comparison, not a mandate for every interaction.
384	- Remove “Replay emphasis”; use a gentle continuous pulse for this page, with a static reduced-motion equivalent. This does not authorize constant pulsing on every page.
385	- Make clear which facts are given and which follow. If a history case says not enrolled, make it explicitly given rather than inferring it from studying history.
386	
387	Content preservation checks for the conditional sequence:
388	- Necessary examples retain only, only if, only when, only where, always, requires, must; explain their meaning in the complete sentence and preserve modality/scope. This is the existing lesson coverage, not a context-free replacement dictionary.
389	- Sufficient Group 1 stays on-page: if; when/whenever; any/anytime; all; every/every time; in order to; people who; each.
390	- Keep the reference-letter qualifiers, including current and accepted for review. A distractor should reveal a reasoning error, not reward guessing.
391	
392	## Must arrow: correction and open work
393	
394	- Confirmed title: **Must arrow**, not “Read the must arrow.”
395	- Teach the direction explicitly with a familiar relationship: in this convention sufficient on the left, necessary on the right, arrow pointing right. The left condition guarantees the right **if the left condition is true**. The arrow alone asserts neither condition as a fact, neither causation nor chronological order, and no reverse inference.
396	- The lead “A must arrow shows what has to be true if another condition is true” was rejected as too abstract on 2026-10-11. Do not preserve it as approved wording. Joshua wants the direction shown and explained directly.
397	- Current provisional implementation: “The must arrow points from left to right. If the left side is true, the right side must be true.” The new LSAT Lab clip (jeDnykOrswY) supports certainty at 0:31 and left requiring right at 8:19, per the source review in this session. This wording is not yet user-approved.
398	- Source comparison completed; final wording remains provisional pending Joshua’s review. Earlier assistant summaries saying tests passed or an opening was updated are implementation reports, not user approval.
399	- Source finding, not a user wording approval: original HTML `raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/conditional_reasoning_transcription.html`, lines 322-356, supports sufficient-to-necessary placement. The parent source comparison is recorded in `brainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum/2026-10-11-must-arrow-review.md`.
400	- Source pointers, relative to the vault resolved through its AGENTS.md: `wiki/sources/lsat-lab-conditional-logic-clip.md` and `corpus/Curriculum/Conditional Logic/Notes/_megadoc_reference_conditionals.md#the-must-arrow-shows-a-direction-of-guarantee`.

403	
404	Status: explicit newest user direction, relayed in this chat; resulting markup is provisional until reviewed by Joshua.
405	
406	- Label the diagram's **Left side** and **Right side** clearly.
407	- Teach how to follow/read this arrow from left to right, and explain **must** as what must be true if the left condition is true.
408	- Simplify or remove the repeated closing student conclusion. Prefer one intuitive readback of the diagram over restating the same inference in several paragraphs.

Preserve the condition that the left side is true. Do not call new markup user-approved merely because it implements this request.

GIF or Kling video is an option, not a requirement; native motion may serve the same purpose. Preserve reduced-motion access and existing spending authorization boundaries.

Walk the practice from start through response, feedback, progress and completion. Confirm rejected labels are absent, actions are clear, and a substantive learning check remains. Do not treat a progress bar alone as proof of mastery, or mark new implementation approved without Joshua's review.

186:## Gold standard: the Contrapositive lesson page (approved 2026-10-12)
## Gold standard: the Contrapositive lesson page (approved 2026-10-12)

Joshua called the finished page "perfect". Treat it as the template for every other concept lecture: same order, same density, same rules. Do not copy its wording or its examples. Rework the structure for the new topic and pick fresh examples.

**Where it lives:** `ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/` (`OpeningCourseContent.tsx` `ContrapositiveNote` and `FourRules`, `ContrapositiveVisuals.tsx`, `contrapositive-visuals.css`), commit `383747e9` plus the uncommitted follow-ups of 2026-10-12. Video project: `ai-os-projects/contrapositive-video/lecture` (`script.json`, `index.html`, `build_audio.py`). Full correction log: `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md`.

### Page shape (about 600 words of text, seven sections)

1. **What it is.** Plain definition first, in a navy panel, then one short example. No run-up.
2. **Why it matters or what it gives you.** Two or three sentences. The key term pulses, one exclamation at most.
3. **Watch the lesson.** A 60 to 90 second narrated video placed after the idea is introduced. Captions track, poster image, written fallback that only renders when opened or when the video fails.
4. **How to write it.** Two bold steps (Step 1 and Step 2), then an interactive demo with "Step 1. Click here to ..." and "Step 2. Click here to ..." buttons. A second demo uses a fresh, simple, intuitive example where one side already starts negated, so the rule that two nots cancel is shown, not stated. Each demo shows the rule as an if-then sentence above the diagram and the result as an if-then sentence below.
5. **What it is not.** One line, then slim cards: red "Wrong" for each look-alike, green "Right" for the answer. Badge, one-line change, diagram. Nothing else.
6. **A short habit paragraph.** One paragraph, no list.
7. **Pop Quiz.** Centered title with a letter-pop and light-sweep effect. Four items, one at a time. Ideas shown as short phrases, never letters. Right answer explanation is one sentence (the answer as a natural if-then). Wrong answer explanation is "Not that one." plus one specific reason about that choice. No intro paragraph, no category tag lines.

### What Joshua wanted (keep)

- One main example carried through the page, plus one fresh second example. Never the same example in more places than that, and never an example already used on another page.
- Natural if-then sentences for every rule and result. Diagrams support the sentence; they never replace it.
- Bold numbered steps. Controls say what to click. The student drives; nothing autoplays.
- Colour carries meaning and travels with the idea (amber sufficient, teal necessary). Wrong looks wrong (red, X); right looks right (green, check).
- Sleek, high-end visuals. A new effect for each new moment; never reuse the same trick. Every animation has a still equivalent and stops under reduced motion.
- Short. When a page feels like too much, cut sections, not words: remove whole parts that repeat what the video, a demo or the quiz already does.
- Ideas in quiz diagrams are condensed statements ("On the list", "Wristband"), not letter codes.
- Narrated video in his voice standard, AI voice acceptable, built from the same wording as the page.

### What Joshua rejected (never repeat)

- A squiggle (~) for negation anywhere. Use the word "not" or a diagonal strike from the idea's top left to bottom right.
- The raffle, coat, coffee, breathing, elevator, door and any example already used elsewhere. "Door", "patterns" and "move" as student words.
- Sophistry and obligation metaphors ("promise", "depends on", "nothing to stand on"). Stock encouragement and run-ups ("Here's the good news", "Every time:").
- Content repeated on the page, a legend that restates what a pill already says, a paragraph before the quiz explaining the quiz, tag lines that add nothing.
- Complicated interactives: the first light-bulb stage (wires, chips, extra buttons) and the "four worlds" picker were both cut as too busy. One idea, one control, one sentence.
- Long answer explanations. Explanations that talk about the diagram ("both ideas get a not") instead of saying the answer in plain English.
- Letters standing in for ideas.

### Process that produced it (work backwards from this)

1. Write the section list above for the new topic before any copy. Cut anything the video, a demo or the quiz already covers.
2. Draft prose in Joshua's voice, then run the `humanizer` skill over it and the corrections log. No em dashes.
3. Build the visuals one at a time, check each on the live preview (not only in tests), and ask what is new about each effect.
4. Pin the page order and the key phrases in the content test; update the test when a section is deliberately cut.
5. Video: one script file, narration synthesized per sentence, word timings for sync, scenes built in the page's own palette, rendered and checked frame by frame. Voice: Kokoro af_heart (author grade A) when no paid voice key exists.
6. Log every new correction in `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` and here before moving on.

### Necessary definition and careful door metaphor, 2026-10-13
Explicit direction: Joshua's wording "The necessary condition is what you can guarantee will occur if the sufficient condition happens." leads the "Where the certainty is" section of the Necessary page. The door metaphor from his source may be used carefully (sufficient opens the door; necessary is guaranteed on the other side). This narrows the earlier blanket rejection of "door" as a student word; "door" as a unit of content stays banned. Avoid copy that sounds AI-written. Acceptance: read the section aloud; the metaphor must not suggest cause, timing, or that the necessary condition is only true behind the door.

117	
118	export type ConditionRole = 'sufficient' | 'necessary';
119	export type SpanCheck = { correct: boolean; roles: (ConditionRole | null)[]; hint: string };
120	
121	const norm = (word: string) => word.replace(/’/g, "'").replace(/[^\p{L}\p{N}'-]/gu, '').toLowerCase();
122	const words = (text: string) => text.split(/\s+/).map(norm).filter(Boolean);
123	// A dropped article, modal or "to" at the edges is not a miss: the learner still found the condition.
124	const LEADING = new Set(['a', 'an', 'the', 'to', 'will', 'can', 'must', 'should', 'is', 'are']);
125	function core(list: string[]) {
126	  let start = 0;
127	  while (start < list.length - 1 && LEADING.has(list[start])) start += 1;
128	  let end = list.length;
129	  if (end - start > 1 && list[end - 1] === 'to') end -= 1;
130	  return list.slice(start, end).join(' ');
131	}
132	const accepted = (entry: string) => entry.split('|').map(text => core(words(text)));
133	
134	export const hasConditionSpans = (statement: string) => statement in SPANS;
135	export function conditionAnswer(statement: string, role: ConditionRole): string {
136	  const entry = SPANS[statement]?.[role === 'sufficient' ? 0 : 1] ?? '';
137	  return entry.split('|')[0].trim();
138	}
139	/** Tokens match how the screen splits the rule: on whitespace, punctuation left attached. */
140	export function spanText(statement: string, span: number[]): string {
141	  const tokens = statement.split(/\s+/);
142	  return tokens.slice(span[0], span[1] + 1).join(' ').replace(/^[^\p{L}\p{N}]+/u, '').replace(/[.,;:!?]+$/, '');
143	}
144	export function roleOfSpan(statement: string, span: number[]): ConditionRole | null {
145	  const entry = SPANS[statement];
146	  if (!entry) return null;
147	  const tokens = statement.split(/\s+/);
148	  const picked = core(tokens.slice(span[0], span[1] + 1).map(norm).filter(Boolean));
149	  if (accepted(entry[0]).includes(picked)) return 'sufficient';
150	  if (accepted(entry[1]).includes(picked)) return 'necessary';
151	  return null;
152	}
153	export function checkConditionSpans(statement: string, spans: number[][]): SpanCheck {
154	  const roles = spans.map(span => roleOfSpan(statement, span));
155	  if (spans.length < 2) return { correct: false, roles, hint: 'Highlight both conditions. Click the first word of a condition, then its last word.' };
156	  if (roles.some(role => !role)) return { correct: false, roles, hint: 'One highlight is not a complete condition. Include every restriction, such as “within 30 days,” and leave the indicator out.' };
157	  if (roles[0] === roles[1]) return { correct: false, roles, hint: 'Both highlights are the same condition. The rule has two different ones.' };
158	  return { correct: true, roles, hint: '' };
159	}
160	
161	/** Test hook: every authored wording must be found in its rule, without overlap. */
162	export function spanEntries() { return Object.entries(SPANS); }
163	export function locateWording(statement: string, wording: string): [number, number] | null {
164	  const tokens = statement.split(/\s+/).map(norm);
165	  const target = words(wording);
166	  for (let start = 0; start + target.length <= tokens.length; start += 1) {
167	    const slice = tokens.slice(start, start + target.length);
168	    if (slice.every((word, i) => word === target[i])) return [start, start + target.length - 1];
169	  }
170	  return null;
171	}

1	import { useEffect, useState } from 'react';
2	import { Check } from 'lucide-react';
3	import { drillDemos, type DemoFrame } from '../content/drillDemos';
4	import './drill-demo.css';
5	
6	const STEP_MS = 5200;
7	
8	function reducedMotion() {
9	  return typeof window !== 'undefined' && !!window.matchMedia?.('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;
10	}
11	
12	function Frame({ frame }: { frame: DemoFrame }) {
13	  if (frame.kind === 'pick') {
14	    let order = 0;
15	    return <div className="dd-pick">
16	      <p className="dd-sentence">{frame.words.map((word, index) => {
17	        const picked = frame.picks.includes(index);
18	        const delay = picked ? 0.7 + 0.7 * order++ : 0;
19	        return <span key={index} className={picked ? 'dd-word dd-picked' : 'dd-word'} style={picked ? { animationDelay: `${delay}s` } : undefined}>{word}</span>;
20	      })}</p>
21	      <span className="dd-chip" style={{ animationDelay: `${0.9 + 0.7 * frame.picks.length}s` }}>{frame.chip}</span>
22	    </div>;
23	  }
24	  if (frame.kind === 'type') return <div className="dd-type">{frame.lines.map((line, index) => <div key={index} className="dd-line">
25	    {line.label && <span className="dd-label">{line.label}</span>}
26	    <span className="dd-field"><span className="dd-typed" style={{ animationDelay: `${0.4 + index * 1.4}s` }}>{line.text}</span></span>
27	  </div>)}</div>;
28	  if (frame.kind === 'place') return <div className="dd-place">
29	    <div className="dd-slot"><span className="dd-label">Enough (left)</span><span className="dd-card dd-card-left">{frame.left}</span></div>
30	    <span className="dd-arrow" aria-hidden="true">→</span>
31	    <div className="dd-slot"><span className="dd-label">Required (right)</span><span className="dd-card dd-card-right">{frame.right}</span></div>
32	  </div>;
33	  return <div className="dd-choose">
34	    <p className="dd-question">{frame.question}</p>
35	    <div className="dd-options">{frame.options.map((option, index) => <span key={index} className={index === frame.correct ? 'dd-option dd-correct' : 'dd-option'}>
36	      <i>{index === frame.correct ? <Check size={13}/> : String.fromCharCode(65 + index)}</i>{option}
37	    </span>)}</div>
38	  </div>;
39	}
40	
41	/** Instruction-page walkthrough: one animated frame per step of this drill, in drill order. */
42	export function DrillDemo({ drillId }: { drillId: string }) {
43	  const demo = drillDemos[drillId];
44	  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);
45	  const [paused, setPaused] = useState(reducedMotion);
46	  useEffect(() => {
47	    if (!demo || paused) return;
48	    const timer = setTimeout(() => setIndex(current => (current + 1) % demo.steps.length), STEP_MS);
49	    return () => clearTimeout(timer);
50	  }, [demo, index, paused]);
51	  if (!demo) return null;
52	  const step = demo.steps[index];
53	  return <section className="dd-demo" aria-label="How this drill works">
54	    <p className="dd-eyebrow">How this drill works</p>
55	    {demo.sentence && <blockquote className="dd-example">Example: {demo.sentence}</blockquote>}
56	    <ol className="dd-steps">{demo.steps.map((item, i) => <li key={item.title}>
57	      <button type="button" aria-current={i === index ? 'step' : undefined} onClick={() => { setIndex(i); setPaused(true); }}><span>{i + 1}</span>{item.title}</button>
58	    </li>)}</ol>
59	    <div className="dd-stage" key={index} aria-live="polite">
60	      <p className="dd-caption"><strong>Step {index + 1}.</strong> {step.caption}</p>
61	      <Frame frame={step.frame}/>
62	    </div>
63	    {demo.steps.length > 1 && <div className="dd-progress" aria-hidden="true"><i key={`${index}-${paused}`} className={paused ? 'dd-bar-paused' : ''} style={{ animationDuration: `${STEP_MS}ms` }}/></div>}
64	  </section>;
65	}

.dd-demo{--dd-ink:#173544;--dd-soft:#5c7079;--dd-line:#d8d2c4;--dd-paper:#fffaf0;--dd-pick:#e9bd74;--dd-right:#1f7a54;--dd-right-bg:#e3f4ea;
  margin:22px 0 26px;padding:20px 22px 0;border:1px solid var(--dd-line);border-radius:14px;background:var(--dd-paper);color:var(--dd-ink);overflow:hidden;text-align:left}
.dd-eyebrow{margin:0 0 8px;font-size:11px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:.15em;text-transform:uppercase;color:var(--dd-soft)}
.dd-example{margin:0 0 14px;padding:0;border:0;font:italic 17px/1.5 Georgia,serif;color:var(--dd-ink)}
.dd-steps{display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:6px;list-style:none;margin:0 0 16px;padding:0}
.dd-steps button{display:inline-flex;align-items:center;gap:7px;border:1px solid var(--dd-line);background:transparent;border-radius:999px;padding:4px 11px 4px 4px;font-size:13px;color:var(--dd-soft);cursor:pointer;transition:background .25s,color .25s,border-color .25s}
.dd-steps button span{display:grid;place-items:center;width:20px;height:20px;border-radius:50%;background:#efe9dc;font-size:11px;font-weight:700}
.dd-steps button[aria-current=step]{background:var(--dd-ink);border-color:var(--dd-ink);color:#fff}
.dd-steps button[aria-current=step] span{background:var(--dd-pick);color:var(--dd-ink)}
.dd-stage{min-height:150px;animation:dd-in .45s cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1)}
.dd-caption{margin:0 0 14px;font-size:15px;line-height:1.5}
@keyframes dd-in{from{opacity:0;transform:translateX(16px)}to{opacity:1;transform:none}}

.dd-sentence{margin:0;font:clamp(19px,1.9vw,23px)/1.8 Georgia,serif}
.dd-word{display:inline-block;margin-right:.28em;padding:0 2px;border-radius:4px}
.dd-picked{animation:dd-pick .35s ease both}
@keyframes dd-pick{from{background:transparent;box-shadow:none}to{background:var(--dd-pick);box-shadow:0 0 0 2px var(--dd-pick)}}
.dd-chip{display:inline-block;margin-top:10px;font-size:14px;font-weight:700;color:var(--dd-right);background:var(--dd-right-bg);border:1px solid var(--dd-right);border-radius:999px;padding:3px 12px;animation:dd-rise .4s ease both}
@keyframes dd-rise{from{opacity:0;transform:translateY(6px)}to{opacity:1;transform:none}}

.dd-type{display:grid;gap:10px}
.dd-line{display:grid;gap:4px}
.dd-label{font-size:12px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:.04em;color:var(--dd-soft)}
.dd-field{display:block;border:1px solid var(--dd-line);border-radius:8px;background:#fff;padding:9px 12px;min-height:42px;font-size:16px}
.dd-typed{display:inline-block;clip-path:inset(0 100% 0 0);animation:dd-type 1.2s steps(28,end) both}
@keyframes dd-type{to{clip-path:inset(0 0 0 0)}}

.dd-place{display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr auto 1fr;align-items:end;gap:12px}
.dd-slot{display:grid;gap:6px}
.dd-card{display:block;min-height:46px;padding:11px 12px;border:1px dashed var(--dd-line);border-radius:8px;background:#fff;font-size:15px}
.dd-card-left{animation:dd-drop-left .6s .6s cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1) both}
.dd-card-right{animation:dd-drop-right .6s 1.4s cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1) both}
@keyframes dd-drop-left{from{opacity:0;transform:translate(-18px,-14px)}to{opacity:1;transform:none;border-style:solid;border-color:var(--dd-right)}}
@keyframes dd-drop-right{from{opacity:0;transform:translate(18px,-14px)}to{opacity:1;transform:none;border-style:solid;border-color:var(--dd-right)}}
.dd-arrow{font-size:22px;padding-bottom:10px;color:var(--dd-soft)}

.dd-question{margin:0 0 10px;font:17px/1.5 Georgia,serif}
.dd-options{display:grid;gap:8px}
.dd-option{display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;padding:9px 12px;border:1px solid var(--dd-line);border-radius:8px;background:#fff;font-size:15px}
.dd-option i{display:grid;place-items:center;width:22px;height:22px;border:1px solid var(--dd-line);border-radius:4px;font-style:normal;font-size:12px;color:var(--dd-soft)}
.dd-correct{animation:dd-right .4s 1.3s ease both}
.dd-correct i{animation:dd-right-mark .4s 1.3s ease both}
@keyframes dd-right{to{border-color:var(--dd-right);background:var(--dd-right-bg);box-shadow:inset 0 0 0 1px var(--dd-right)}}
@keyframes dd-right-mark{to{background:var(--dd-right);border-color:var(--dd-right);color:#fff}}

.dd-progress{height:3px;margin:16px -22px 0;background:#efe9dc}
.dd-progress i{display:block;height:100%;width:100%;background:var(--dd-pick);transform-origin:left;animation:dd-bar linear both}
.dd-progress i.dd-bar-paused{animation:none;transform:scaleX(0)}
@keyframes dd-bar{from{transform:scaleX(0)}to{transform:scaleX(1)}}

@media(max-width:600px){.dd-place{grid-template-columns:1fr}.dd-arrow{display:none}}
@media(prefers-reduced-motion:reduce){.dd-stage,.dd-picked,.dd-chip,.dd-typed,.dd-card-left,.dd-card-right,.dd-correct,.dd-correct i{animation-duration:1ms!important;animation-delay:0s!important}}
.dark .dd-demo{--dd-ink:#edf4ed;--dd-soft:#b5c9cc;--dd-line:#4f666d;--dd-paper:#1e3741;--dd-right-bg:#1d4a38;--dd-right:#5fc495}
.dark .dd-field,.dark .dd-card,.dark .dd-option{background:#152b36}.dark .dd-picked{color:#173544}
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { OPENING_COURSE_PATH } from '../openingCoursePath';
import { drillDemos } from './drillDemos';
import { getSourceWorksheetDrill } from './sourceWorksheetDrills';

const stagesFor: Record<string, number> = { identify: 2, roles: 2, forward: 3, rephrase: 3, contrapositive: 7 };

describe('drill instruction walkthroughs', () => {
  const drills = OPENING_COURSE_PATH.filter(step => step.type === 'drill');

  it('gives every drill on the path its own walkthrough with a distinct example', () => {
    for (const drill of drills) expect(drillDemos[drill.id], drill.id).toBeDefined();
    const examples = drills.map(drill => drillDemos[drill.id].sentence).filter(Boolean);
    expect(new Set(examples).size).toBe(examples.length);
  });

  it('matches each worksheet drill step for step, without using one of its own sentences', () => {
    for (const drill of drills) {
      const set = getSourceWorksheetDrill(drill.id);
      if (!set) continue;
      const demo = drillDemos[drill.id];
      expect(demo.steps, drill.id).toHaveLength(stagesFor[set.taskKind]);
      expect(set.items.map(item => item.statement), drill.id).not.toContain(demo.sentence);
    }
  });
});
import { expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { checkConditionSpans, locateWording, roleOfSpan, spanEntries, spanText } from './conditionSpans';
import { getSourceWorksheetDrill, sourceWorksheetDrills } from './sourceWorksheetDrills';
import { OPENING_COURSE_PATH } from '../openingCoursePath';

// Revisits reuse another set's items under a task that adds the highlight step, so they count too.
const allSets = [...sourceWorksheetDrills, ...OPENING_COURSE_PATH.map(step => getSourceWorksheetDrill(step.id)).filter(set => set !== undefined)];
const withConditions = allSets.filter(set => ['identify', 'forward', 'roles', 'contrapositive'].includes(set!.taskKind));

it('covers every rule that has a conditions step', () => {
  const known = new Set(spanEntries().map(([statement]) => statement));
  const missing = withConditions.flatMap(set => set!.items).filter(item => !known.has(item.statement)).map(item => item.id);
  expect(missing).toEqual([]);
});

it('finds each authored wording inside its rule, and the two conditions never overlap', () => {
  for (const [statement, [sufficient, necessary]] of spanEntries()) {
    const found = [sufficient, necessary].map(entry => entry.split('|').map(text => locateWording(statement, text.trim())));
    for (const group of found) for (const place of group) expect(place, statement).not.toBeNull();
    const [s, n] = found.map(group => group[0]!);
    expect(s[1] < n[0] || n[1] < s[0], statement).toBe(true);
    expect(roleOfSpan(statement, s), statement).toBe('sufficient');
    expect(roleOfSpan(statement, n), statement).toBe('necessary');
  }
});

it('accepts either order, a dropped article, and rejects a span that swallows the indicator', () => {
  const rule = 'A warranty is valid if the product is registered within 30 days.';
  expect(checkConditionSpans(rule, [[0, 3], [5, 11]]).correct).toBe(true);
  expect(checkConditionSpans(rule, [[1, 3], [4, 11]]).correct).toBe(false);
  expect(checkConditionSpans(rule, [[5, 11], [1, 3]]).correct).toBe(true);
  expect(checkConditionSpans(rule, [[0, 3], [4, 11]]).correct).toBe(false);
  expect(checkConditionSpans(rule, [[0, 3]]).correct).toBe(false);
  expect(spanText(rule, [5, 11])).toBe('the product is registered within 30 days');
});

export type OpeningCourseStep = {
  id: string;
  type: 'note' | 'drill';
  title: string;
  description: string;
  alsoAvailableIn?: Array<'orientation' | 'sufficient-necessary' | 'group-1' | 'group-2'>;
  unitId: 'orientation' | 'sufficient-necessary' | 'group-1' | 'group-2';
};

// Reading destinations are not legacy completion IDs. Visiting one records no completion.
export const OPENING_COURSE_PATH: OpeningCourseStep[] = [
  { id: 'meaning', type: 'note', title: 'What conditionals are', description: 'Separate a rule from a fact, and see what a conditional actually promises.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'sufficient', type: 'note', title: 'The Sufficient Condition', description: 'When one condition guarantees another.', unitId: 'sufficient-necessary', alsoAvailableIn: ['orientation'] },
  { id: 'necessary', type: 'note', title: 'The Necessary Condition', description: 'What the sufficient condition requires.', unitId: 'sufficient-necessary', alsoAvailableIn: ['orientation'] },
  { id: 'must-arrow', type: 'note', title: 'Must arrow', description: 'What the arrow says, where to start reading it, and what it is for.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'foundation-practice', type: 'drill', title: 'Spot the condition', description: 'Identify sufficient and necessary conditions, place them beside the arrow, recognize indicators, and decide what the facts prove.', unitId: 'sufficient-necessary', alsoAvailableIn: ['orientation'] },
  { id: 'sufficient-words', type: 'note', title: 'Find the complete condition', description: 'Identify the full sufficient condition, including the words that limit who or what the rule covers.', unitId: 'group-1' },
  { id: 'sufficient-practice', type: 'drill', title: 'Build the sufficient side', description: 'Find the whole condition, construct the diagram and repair its direction.', unitId: 'group-1' },
  { id: 'necessary-words', type: 'note', title: 'Necessary-condition words', description: 'Read only, only if, only when, only where, always, requires and must.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'whole-meaning', type: 'note', title: 'Keep the whole meaning', description: 'Keep qualifications, permission and obligation intact before translating a rule.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-intro-identify', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 2: Find both conditions', description: 'Find the indicators and their groups, then write the complete sufficient and necessary conditions.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-roles', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 3: Place the conditions', description: 'Put each condition on its side of the arrow.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-intro-diagrams', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 4: Build the diagram', description: 'Return to the introductory sentences and build each diagram.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-rephrase', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 5: Write it as if/then', description: 'Rewrite each rule as if/then without changing its meaning, then compare your wording with the explanation.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'mixed-practice', type: 'drill', title: 'Conditional translation practice', description: 'Who or what does the rule cover? What does it require? Answer without adding anything the rule does not say.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'contrapositive', type: 'note', title: 'Contrapositive', description: 'What it is, how to write it, and what it is not.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'four-situations', type: 'note', title: 'What the rule tells you', description: 'Four situations: two settle something, two settle nothing.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'source-intro-diagrams-contrapositive', type: 'drill', title: 'Revisit Drill 4: Add the contrapositive', description: 'Add the contrapositive to the rules you already know.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-rephrase-contrapositive', type: 'drill', title: 'Revisit Drill 5: Negate the whole condition', description: 'Complete the contrapositive for each rewritten rule. Keep every qualification.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'contrapositive-practice', type: 'drill', title: 'Check your contrapositive', description: 'Choose the correct transformation, including rules that already contain negative conditions.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'source-full-translation', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 6: Build both diagrams', description: 'Build the original and the contrapositive. Keep each complete condition intact.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-independent', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 7: Put it into practice', description: 'Run the full process on your own. Work in short sessions and return where you left off.', unitId: 'group-2' },
];

export function openingCourseStep(id?: string) {
  return OPENING_COURSE_PATH.find(step => step.id === id);
}

// Alternatives name legacy units that already teach this complete concept.
export function openingStepAccessUnits(step: OpeningCourseStep) {
  return [step.unitId, ...(step.alsoAvailableIn ?? [])];
}

/** Reviewed derivatives of Joshua's Group 1/2 worksheets. Original source text and hashes are retained.
 * NOT (...) negates the entire proposition, including qualifiers and compound predicates.
 * Sets before contraposition contain its reference data for later use; the UI must gate its display by taskKind.
 */
export type SourceWorksheetTaskKind = 'identify' | 'indicators' | 'forward' | 'roles' | 'rephrase' | 'contrapositive';
export interface SourceWorksheetItem {
  id: string; sourceId: string; originalStatement: string; statement: string;
  indicators: { text: string; group: 1 | 2 }[];
  sufficient: string; necessary: string; diagram: string; contrapositive: string;
  ifThen: string; contrapositiveWords: string; explanation: string; correctionNotes: string[];
  source: { file: string; sha256: string; part: string; item: number };
}
export interface SourceWorksheetDrillSet {
  id: string; title: string; shortTitle: string; stage: string; taskKind: SourceWorksheetTaskKind;
  instructions: string; items: SourceWorksheetItem[];
}
export const sourceWorksheetDrills: SourceWorksheetDrillSet[] = [
  {
    "id": "source-intro-identify",
    "title": "Find the indicators and both conditions",
    "shortTitle": "Intro training",
    "stage": "Recognize",
    "taskKind": "identify",
    "instructions": "Select the indicators and choose each one’s group. Then highlight the two complete conditions in the rule. Keep words that limit who, when, or what the rule covers.",
    "items": [
      {
        "id": "source-intro-identify-01",
        "sourceId": "intro-p1-01",
        "originalStatement": "A warranty is valid if the product is registered within 30 days.",
        "statement": "A warranty is valid if the product is registered within 30 days.",
        "indicators": [
          {
            "text": "if",
            "group": 1
          }
        ],
        "sufficient": "the product is registered within 30 days",
        "necessary": "the warranty is valid",
        "diagram": "the product is registered within 30 days → the warranty is valid",
        "contrapositive": "NOT (the warranty is valid) → NOT (the product is registered within 30 days)",
        "ifThen": "If the product is registered within 30 days, then the warranty is valid.",
        "contrapositiveWords": "If the warranty is not valid, then the product is not registered within 30 days.",
        "explanation": "The deadline belongs inside the sufficient condition. Registration after 30 days does not meet this trigger.",
        "correctionNotes": [],
        "source": {
          "file": "Intro training set/Homework/2025-06-23_intro training group ½ (3).pdf",
          "sha256": "93623f4304af75a3c2e009610dfa0fc9ec5316ed3c304c67d6d2166d48c19a4d",
          "part": "Intro training · Part 1",
          "item": 1
        }
      },
      {
        "id": "source-intro-identify-02",
        "sourceId": "intro-p1-02",
        "originalStatement": "Whenever you see lightning, expect thunder to follow.",
        "statement": "Whenever you see lightning, you should expect thunder to follow.",
        "indicators": [
          {
            "text": "Whenever",
            "group": 1
          }
        ],
        "sufficient": "you see lightning",
        "necessary": "you should expect thunder to follow",
        "diagram": "you see lightning → you should expect thunder to follow",
        "contrapositive": "NOT (you should expect thunder to follow) → NOT (you see lightning)",
        "ifThen": "If you see lightning, then you should expect thunder to follow.",
        "contrapositiveWords": "If it is not true that you should expect thunder to follow, then you do not see lightning.",
        "explanation": "The result is that you should expect thunder. The rule does not promise that thunder actually follows.",
        "correctionNotes": [
          "The key strengthens “expect thunder” to “thunder will follow.” Preserve the expectation.",

          "file": "Intro training set/Homework/2025-06-23_intro training group ½ (3).pdf",
  }
];

// Later visits reuse source items without counting them as new worksheet questions.
const contrapositiveRevisits: Record<string, { sourceSetId: string; title: string; shortTitle: string }> = {
  'source-intro-diagrams-contrapositive': {
    sourceSetId: 'source-intro-diagrams',
    title: 'Revisit the intro: build the contrapositive',
    shortTitle: 'Intro training: contrapositives',
  },
  'source-rephrase-contrapositive': {
    sourceSetId: 'source-rephrase',
    title: 'Revisit the rules: write both directions',
    shortTitle: 'Rephrasing: contrapositives',
  },
};

export const getSourceWorksheetDrill = (id: string): SourceWorksheetDrillSet | undefined => {
  const canonical = sourceWorksheetDrills.find((set) => set.id === id);
  if (canonical) return canonical;
  const revisit = contrapositiveRevisits[id];
  if (!revisit) return undefined;
  const source = sourceWorksheetDrills.find((set) => set.id === revisit.sourceSetId);
  if (!source) return undefined;
  return {
    ...source,
    id,
    title: revisit.title,
    shortTitle: revisit.shortTitle,
    stage: 'After contraposition',
    taskKind: 'contrapositive',
    instructions: 'Return to these familiar sentences. Find the indicators, write the complete conditions, and define your symbols. Build the original diagram and compare it with ours, then build the contrapositive and check it. Last, write both in ordinary if/then language. Reverse the conditions and negate each complete idea, including its restrictions.',
  };
};

/** A run of adjacent words the learner selected, with the group they chose ('1', '2' or ''). */
export interface IndicatorSelection { text: string; group: string }
export interface IndicatorCheck { correct: boolean; hint: string }

export const normalizeIndicator = (text: string) => text.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9 ]/g, '').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();

// Accepted alternatives. Items keep one keyed indicator; these rules decide which other selections mean the same thing.
const DETERMINER_TAILS = ['those', 'the ones', 'these'];
const QUANTIFIERS = ['any', 'every', 'all', 'each', 'no', 'anyone', 'anything', 'everyone', 'everything', 'none'];
const WORD_ALTERNATIVES: Record<string, string[]> = { every: ['each and every'] };
const WHO_PHRASE = /^([a-z]+ )?who$/;

function acceptsSelection(expected: string, text: string) {
  if (text === expected || WORD_ALTERNATIVES[expected]?.includes(text)) return true;
  for (const tail of DETERMINER_TAILS) {
    // "Only those" was taught as "only": accept the head word, and the phrase continued by "who".
    if (expected.endsWith(` ${tail}`) && (text === expected.slice(0, -tail.length - 1) || text === `${expected} who`)) return true;
  }
  // A condition introduced by who: accept "who" or "[group] who" (e.g. "members who").
  if ((expected === 'who' || expected.endsWith(' who')) && WHO_PHRASE.test(text)) return true;
  // "Every applicant who" selected as one run still marks the quantifier.
  const words = text.split(' ');
  return QUANTIFIERS.includes(expected) && words.length <= 4 && words[0] === expected && words[words.length - 1] === 'who';
}

const containsRun = (outer: string, inner: string) => ` ${outer} `.includes(` ${inner} `);

/** Grades the indicator step and returns a hint that never names the keyed answer. */
export function checkIndicatorSelection(item: Pick<SourceWorksheetItem, 'statement' | 'indicators'>, selections: IndicatorSelection[]): IndicatorCheck {
  const expected = item.indicators.map(i => ({ text: normalizeIndicator(i.text), group: String(i.group) }));
  const remaining = [...expected];
  const statementWords = normalizeIndicator(item.statement).split(' ');
  const whoIntroducesCondition = statementWords.includes('who') && expected.some(e => e.group === '1' && QUANTIFIERS.includes(e.text));
  const extra: string[] = [];
  const wrongGroup: string[] = [];
  for (const selection of selections) {
    const text = normalizeIndicator(selection.text);
    const index = remaining.findIndex(e => acceptsSelection(e.text, text));
    if (index >= 0) {
      if (remaining[index].group !== selection.group) wrongGroup.push(selection.text);
      remaining.splice(index, 1);
      continue;
    }
    // Two adjacent indicators ("must always") form one run of selected words.
    const pairs: [number, number][] = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < remaining.length; i++) for (let j = 0; j < remaining.length; j++) if (i !== j && `${remaining[i].text} ${remaining[j].text}` === text) pairs.push([i, j]);
    const pair = pairs[0];
    if (pair) {
      if (remaining[pair[0]].group !== selection.group || remaining[pair[1]].group !== selection.group) wrongGroup.push(selection.text);
      for (const i of [...pair].sort((a, b) => b - a)) remaining.splice(i, 1);
      continue;
    }
    if (whoIntroducesCondition && WHO_PHRASE.test(text)) {
      if (selection.group !== '1') wrongGroup.push(selection.text);
      continue;
    }
    extra.push(selection.text);
  }
  if (extra.length) {
    const text = extra[0];
    const normal = normalizeIndicator(text);
    if (expected.some(e => e.text !== normal && containsRun(e.text, normal))) return { correct: false, hint: `“${text}” is only part of an indicator. Select every word of the phrase.` };
    if (expected.some(e => containsRun(normal, e.text))) return { correct: false, hint: `“${text}” includes words that are not part of the indicator. Select only the signal words.` };
    return { correct: false, hint: `“${text}” is not an indicator in this rule.` };
  }
  if (wrongGroup.length) return { correct: false, hint: `Check the group for “${wrongGroup[0]}”. Does it introduce what is enough, or what is required?` };
  if (remaining.length) return { correct: false, hint: remaining.length === 1 ? 'One indicator is still unselected. Read the whole rule again.' : `${remaining.length} indicators are still unselected. Read the whole rule again.` };
  return { correct: true, hint: '' };
}

src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/ConditionalOpeningLesson.tsx:581:        "This is why the only belongs with our sufficient indicators: the complete group it introduces gives us the starting condition. Read it as a whole phrase. We'll compare it with only once we've learned the other group.",
src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/ConditionalOpeningLesson.tsx:622:        "Compare The only people admitted are members. That also tells us every admitted person is a member. The only points to admitted people, the sufficient group. Only members are admitted points to members, the necessary group. Different grammatical groups; the same complete rule.",
src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/ConditionalOpeningLesson.tsx:862:        <p>The only what? The only people admitted. The complete group is admitted people. If someone is admitted, the rule tells us they're a member.</p>

import { cleanup, fireEvent, render, screen, within } from '@testing-library/react';
import { afterEach, beforeEach, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { MemoryRouter } from 'react-router-dom';
import { SourceWorksheetDrill } from './SourceWorksheetDrill';
import { sourceWorksheetDrills } from '../content/sourceWorksheetDrills';

beforeEach(() => localStorage.clear());
afterEach(cleanup);
const run = (setId: string) => render(<SourceWorksheetDrill setId={setId} run/>);
const write = (name: string, value: string) => fireEvent.change(screen.getByLabelText(name), { target: { value } });
const click = (name: string) => fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name }));
const compare = () => click('Compare this step');
const confirm = () => click('I compared the meaning');
// Highlights a condition by the position of its first and last word in the rule.
const mark = (from: number, to: number) => {
  const words = within(screen.getByRole('group', { name: /Click the first and last word/ })).getAllByRole('button');
  fireEvent.click(words[from]);
  fireEvent.click(words[to]);
};

it('keeps intro identification faithful: indicators first, then the learner highlights both conditions', () => {
  run('source-intro-identify');
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Check this step' })).toBeDisabled();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('textbox')).toBeNull();
  click('if');
  fireEvent.change(screen.getByLabelText(/Which group/), { target: { value: '1' } });
  click('Check this step');
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'That step works.' })).toHaveFocus();
  click('Continue');
  expect(screen.queryByLabelText(/Which group/)).toBeNull();
  expect(screen.queryByText('the product is registered within 30 days', { exact: true })).toBeNull();

  expect(screen.queryByText(ours.diagram)).toBeNull();

  expect(screen.queryByText(ours.contrapositive)).toBeNull();

});

import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { checkIndicatorSelection, getSourceWorksheetDrill, sourceWorksheetDrills } from './sourceWorksheetDrills';

describe('reviewed worksheet source bank', () => {
  const items = sourceWorksheetDrills.flatMap((set) => set.items);

  it('includes all six source sets and all 133 distinct task instances', () => {
    // The indicator set (13 sentences) is folded into the intro identification set, keeping its item ids.
    expect(sourceWorksheetDrills.map((set) => set.items.length)).toEqual([28, 15, 10, 25, 20, 35]);
    expect(items).toHaveLength(133);
    expect(new Set(items.map((item) => item.id)).size).toBe(133);
    expect(new Set(items.map((item) => item.sourceId)).size).toBe(133);
    for (const set of sourceWorksheetDrills) {
      const parts = [...new Set(set.items.map((item) => `${item.source.file}|${item.source.part}`))];
      for (const part of parts) {
        const numbers = set.items.filter((item) => `${item.source.file}|${item.source.part}` === part).map((item) => item.source.item);
        expect(numbers).toEqual(Array.from({ length: numbers.length }, (_, index) => index + 1));
      }
    }
  });

  it('retains source text and provenance for every reviewed derivative', () => {
    for (const item of items) {
      expect(item.originalStatement.length).toBeGreaterThan(10);
      expect(item.source.sha256).toMatch(/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/);
      expect(item.source.file).toMatch(/\.(pdf|docx)$/);
      expect(item.explanation.length).toBeGreaterThan(30);
      if (item.statement !== item.originalStatement) {
        expect(item.correctionNotes.some((note) => note.startsWith('Reviewed derivative:'))).toBe(true);
      }
    }
  });

  it('keeps complete propositions when reversing and negating', () => {
    for (const item of items) {
      expect(item.diagram).toBe(`${item.sufficient} → ${item.necessary}`);
      expect(item.contrapositive).toBe(`NOT (${item.necessary}) → NOT (${item.sufficient})`);
    }
    const compound = getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-independent')!.items[24];
    expect(compound.contrapositive).toContain('NOT (people both buy less and use less');
  });

  it('keeps repeated intro sources aligned while giving each task its own identity', () => {
    const recognition = getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-intro-identify')!.items;
    const diagrams = getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-intro-diagrams')!.items;
    diagrams.forEach((item, index) => {
      expect(recognition[index].originalStatement).toBe(item.originalStatement);
      expect(recognition[index].statement).toBe(item.statement);
      expect(recognition[index].diagram).toBe(item.diagram);
      expect(recognition[index].id).not.toBe(item.id);
    });
  });

  it('repairs flagged key errors without silently replacing source evidence', () => {
    const independent = getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-independent')!.items;
    expect(independent[19].necessary).not.toBe('Always');
    expect(independent[27].sufficient).toContain('good');
    expect(independent[27].necessary).toContain('pleasure');
    expect(independent[30].originalStatement).toContain('plant soil decreases');
    expect(independent[30].statement).toContain('holds water longer');
    expect(independent[32].indicators).toEqual([{ text: 'anyone', group: 1 }]);
    expect(independent[32].necessary).toContain('after a week');
    const rephrase = getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-rephrase')!.items;
    expect(rephrase[7].sufficient).toContain('can apply');
    expect(rephrase[17].necessary).toContain('currently active');
    expect(getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-intro-identify')!.items[3].necessary).toContain('required');
  });

  it('keeps modal claims and corrects reviewed indicator classifications', () => {
    for (const id of ['source-intro-identify', 'source-intro-diagrams']) {
      expect(getSourceWorksheetDrill(id)!.items[8].necessary).toBe('the building should be evacuated immediately');
    }
    const rephrase = getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-rephrase')!.items;
    expect(rephrase[3].indicators).toEqual([{ text: 'The only', group: 1 }]);
    // "Every" does not mark the sufficient side here (legally binding does), so only "must" is keyed.
    expect(rephrase[10].indicators).toEqual([{ text: 'must', group: 2 }]);
    expect(rephrase[10].sufficient).toBe('an agreement is legally binding');
    expect(rephrase[6].necessary).toBe('that project involves effective teamwork');
    expect(rephrase[19].necessary).toContain('is required to be recalibrated under the maintenance policy');
    expect(rephrase[19].contrapositiveWords).toContain('is not required to be recalibrated');
    expect(rephrase[19].statement).not.toBe(rephrase[19].originalStatement);
    expect(getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-full-translation')!.items[8].necessary).toBe('system maintenance is performed');
  });

  it('reads simple and negative contrapositives naturally without losing scope', () => {
    const full = getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-full-translation')!.items;
    expect(full[0].contrapositiveWords).toBe('If that intern is not offered a full-time position, then that person is not an intern who completes the program.');
    expect(full[12].contrapositiveWords).toBe('If alarms deactivate on their own, then the emergency protocol initiates within one minute.');
    expect(full[15].contrapositiveWords).toContain('not verified by both authorities');
    const independent = getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-independent')!.items;
    expect(independent[20].contrapositiveWords).toBe('If Rufus is around, then food is not in the fridge.');
    expect(independent[22].contrapositiveWords).toBe('If I can see Arun, then Arun is not in the next room.');
    expect(independent[24].contrapositiveWords).toContain('do not both buy less and use less');
    expect(independent[19].statement).not.toContain('every moment');
  });

  it('binds both sides of each contrapositive to the same case', () => {
    const location = getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-intro-identify')!.items.find((item) => item.id === 'source-indicators-12')!;
    expect(location.contrapositiveWords).toBe('If pathways do not lead to that location, then the park is not accessible at that location.');
    const independent = getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-independent')!.items;
    expect(independent[25].contrapositiveWords).toBe('If a place does not have a good legal system, then that place does not have independent judges.');
    expect(independent[27].contrapositiveWords).toBe('If it is not pleasure, then it is not good under the stated theory.');
  });

  it('restores later contrapositive operations without duplicating canonical source items', () => {
    for (const sourceId of ['source-intro-diagrams', 'source-rephrase']) {
      const source = getSourceWorksheetDrill(sourceId)!;
      const revisit = getSourceWorksheetDrill(`${sourceId}-contrapositive`)!;
      expect(revisit.id).toBe(`${sourceId}-contrapositive`);
      expect(revisit.taskKind).toBe('contrapositive');
      expect(revisit.stage).toBe('After contraposition');
      expect(revisit.items).toBe(source.items);
      expect(revisit.items.map((item) => item.sourceId)).toEqual(source.items.map((item) => item.sourceId));
      expect(source.taskKind).not.toBe('contrapositive');
    }
    expect(sourceWorksheetDrills).toHaveLength(6);
    expect(getSourceWorksheetDrill('source-indicators')).toBeUndefined();
    expect(sourceWorksheetDrills.flatMap((set) => set.items)).toHaveLength(133);
    expect(getSourceWorksheetDrill('unknown')).toBeUndefined();
  });
});

describe('indicator check', () => {
  const find = (setId: string, id: string) => getSourceWorksheetDrill(setId)!.items.find((item) => item.id === id)!;
  const check = (item: Parameters<typeof checkIndicatorSelection>[0], ...selections: [string, string][]) =>
    checkIndicatorSelection(item, selections.map(([text, group]) => ({ text, group })));

  it('accepts who, or a group plus who, where who introduces the condition', () => {
    const members = find('source-intro-identify', 'source-intro-identify-08');
    expect(check(members, ['who', '1']).correct).toBe(true);
    expect(check(members, ['Members who', '1']).correct).toBe(true);
    const applicant = find('source-intro-identify', 'source-intro-identify-13');
    expect(check(applicant, ['Every', '1']).correct).toBe(true);
    expect(check(applicant, ['Every', '1'], ['who', '1']).correct).toBe(true);
    expect(check(applicant, ['Every', '1'], ['applicant who', '1']).correct).toBe(true);
    expect(check(applicant, ['Every applicant who', '1']).correct).toBe(true);
    expect(check(applicant, ['who', '1']).correct).toBe(false);
    const student = find('source-intro-identify', 'source-intro-identify-05');
    expect(check(student, ['any', '1'], ['who', '1']).correct).toBe(true);
    expect(check(student, ['any', '1'], ['who', '2']).correct).toBe(false);
  });

  it('accepts the taught head word of a multi-word indicator', () => {
    const rebate = find('source-rephrase', 'source-rephrase-03');
    expect(check(rebate, ['Only', '2']).correct).toBe(true);
    expect(check(rebate, ['Only those', '2']).correct).toBe(true);
    expect(check(rebate, ['Only those who', '2']).correct).toBe(true);
    const training = find('source-intro-identify', 'source-indicators-01');
    expect(check(training, ['Only', '2']).correct).toBe(false);
    expect(check(training, ['Only if', '2']).correct).toBe(true);
    const q = find('source-independent', 'source-independent-02');
    expect(check(q, ['every', '1']).correct).toBe(true);
    expect(check(q, ['Each and every', '1']).correct).toBe(true);
    const errors = find('source-intro-identify', 'source-indicators-11');
    expect(check(errors, ['must always', '2']).correct).toBe(true);
  });

  it('gives a hint about the chosen words without naming the answer', () => {
    const training = find('source-intro-identify', 'source-indicators-01');
    expect(check(training, ['Only', '2']).hint).toBe('“Only” is only part of an indicator. Select every word of the phrase.');
    expect(check(training, ['Only if', '1']).hint).toMatch(/^Check the group for “Only if”/);
    expect(check(training, ['employees', '1']).hint).toBe('“employees” is not an indicator in this rule.');
    const errors = find('source-intro-identify', 'source-indicators-11');
    expect(check(errors, ['must', '2']).hint).toBe('One indicator is still unselected. Read the whole rule again.');
  });
});
